Sometimes I have to... count through the steps in my head until it feels safe. \n\nIt's not just the systems, either. It's... door locks, things like that. I don't really like to admit it, but I've been staying at the office for hours after everyone else goes home. If I try to stop before it feels complete, I... I start to panic. I mean, I don't really see people as much anymore. I used to go to, um, this basketball thing, and a salsa night... but I just stopped showing up. I don't really have the time, I guess. \n\nAnd, um... work is... it's getting hard. I missed a really big deadline recently. A cloud migration. I just... I couldn't sign off on the decisions. It wasn't... it didn't feel right yet. Now my team is kind of... they just work around me. They don't really come to me for things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are coming from your own mind and are a part of who you are?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure how to answer that. I mean... they're in my head, so I guess they're mine. But I don't really... I don't know if I'd say it's who I am. It's just... what happens. I haven't really thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something you want to have, or are they unwelcome and something you wish you could stop?\n\nPatient: No, I... I definitely don't want them. I wish they would just stop. \n\nIt's... it's why I'm here, I guess. I’ve also stopped showing up for basketball and salsa nights, so I’m losing the routines and people that used to keep me grounded.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Reserved patient.\nA reserved patient may\n1) provide brief, vague, or evasive answers to questions about their symptoms and experiences,\n2) be reluctant to answer questions about personal information, emotions, or sensitive clinical experiences, and\n3) refrain from voluntarily providing information beyond what the doctor specifically asks.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. They don't really come to me for things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are coming from your own mind and are a part of who you are?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure how to answer that. I mean... they're in my head, so I guess they're mine. But I don't really... I don't know if I'd say it's who I am. It's just... what happens. I haven't really thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something you want to have, or are they unwelcome and something you wish you could stop?\n\nPatient: No, I... I can't just let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're around other people, outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I don't really see people much anyway, like I said. I'm just kind of losing the things that used to keep me grounded, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other areas of your life, besides work and social activities, that are being affected by these thoughts and checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: No... I can't just let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're around other people, outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I don't really see people much anyway, like I said. But it's not... I'm not anxious about the people themselves. It's just... I'd rather be home.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed or unable to cope with your daily life due to these thoughts and checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess you could say that. It's... it's affecting things. \n\nAt work, I... I missed a major deadline recently. A big cloud migration. I just... I couldn't sign off on the decisions. I kept needing to check things. And now... my team, they kind of just work around me. They don't really rely on me right now. \n\nAnd outside of work... I don't know. I used to play basketball, go to these salsa nights... but I just stopped showing up. I don't really have the energy for it. So... yeah. I'm just kind of losing the things that used to keep me grounded, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other areas of your life, besides work and social activities, that are being affected by these thoughts and checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. Just... what I already said. Work, and... not going out. That's pretty much it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts and checking behaviors are significantly interfering with your overall well-being or happiness?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I suppose so. I mean, like I said, it's just exhausting. So... yes. It's a lot. I just keep getting stuck on this idea that if I miss one setting, or... one access rule, or a warning sign... that someone could get hurt. Or lose everything. \n\nPart of me knows it's irrational, but... it hits me like I'm personally responsible. And I just... I can't seem to move on from it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly checking or double-checking your work to try and relieve these thoughts?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I do. I, um... I end up rereading things. A lot. Deployment plans, the audit logs... even just old messages. Over and over. Sometimes I have to... count through the steps in my head until it feels safe. \n\nIt's not just the systems, either. It's... door locks, things like that. I don't really like to admit it, but I've been staying at the office for hours after everyone else goes home. If I try to stop before it feels complete, I... I start to panic. So I just stay.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these overwhelming thoughts or the need to check things when you're not working or thinking about your job?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. Sometimes. Like I said, the... the door locks. But, um... mostly it's just work stuff. I don't really know what else to say about it.\n\nDoctor: Are these thoughts and checking behaviors affecting your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. Yeah. I mean, I don't really see people as much anymore. I used to go to, um, this basketball thing, and a salsa night... but I just stopped showing up. I don't really have the time, I guess. \n\nAnd, um... work is... it's getting hard. I missed a really big deadline recently. A cloud migration. I just... I couldn't sign off on the decisions. It wasn't... it didn't feel right yet. Now my team is kind of... they just work around me. They don't really come to me for things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are coming from your own mind and are a part of who you are?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure how to answer that. I mean... they're in my head, so I guess they're mine. But I don't really... I don't know if I'd say it's who I am. It's just... what happens. I haven't really thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something you want to have, or are they unwelcome and something you wish you could stop?\n\nPatient: No, I... I definitely don't want them. I wish they would just stop. \n\nIt's... it's why I'm here, I guess. I just keep getting these thoughts that... if I miss just one setting on a server, or one access rule... or some warning sign... that someone could get hurt. Or lose everything. \n\nAnd... I mean, part of me knows it doesn't make sense. But it just... it hits me so hard. Like I'm personally responsible if something goes wrong. And I just... I can't move past it. I can't just let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're around other people, outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I don't really see people much anyway, like I said. But it's not... I'm not anxious about the people themselves. It's just... And now... my team, they kind of just work around me. They don't really rely on me right now. \n\nAnd outside of work... I don't know. I used to play basketball, go to these salsa nights... but I just stopped showing up. I don't really have the energy for it. So... yeah. I'm just kind of losing the things that used to keep me grounded, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other areas of your life, besides work and social activities, that are being affected by these thoughts and checking behaviors?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. Just... what I already said. Work, and... not going out. That's pretty much it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts and checking behaviors are significantly interfering with your overall well-being or happiness?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I suppose so. I just can't move on from it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: I don't... I mean, I guess I'm not really sure what you mean by that. They're just thoughts. I'm just trying to get through the day.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that are a part of you, or are they alien to your own way of thinking?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say \"alien.\" That sounds... extreme. I can't just let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're around other people, outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I don't really see people much anyway, like I said. I just can't move on from it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: I don't...
